    TL;DR · the day in one read
    1. 01PHP2Shell: CISA lists both flaws as exploited after honeypot code execution; update the runtime, not the CMS. The PHP project released 8.5.12, 8.4.27, 8.3.36 and 8.2.35 on 2026-10-21 to fix CVE-2026-109618 and CVE-2026-109619 (CVSS 4.0 9.2), two flaws in the runtime that allow unauthenticated remote code execution as the web worker account, most directly on Apache mod_php, with no upstream release for PHP 8.1 and older and a third path, CVE-2026-115560, left unfixed (keep session.upload_progress.enabled=0). CISA added both flaws to its Known Exploited Vulnerabilities catalog on 2026-10-26, after a honeypot operator documented 35 successful code executions on PHP 8.4.26 under single-worker mod_php and VulnCheck extended its working exploit across builds of PHP 8.2 to 8.5; Palo Alto Networks' advisory now reports active exploitation against internet-exposed PAN-OS management interfaces, Dream Research recovered a custom build chained with the Linux kernel flaw CVE-2026-46300 that rooted 24 firewalls of one unnamed vendor and attacked a US government-affiliated network, SRF links a data theft at TCS Camping's booking-system supplier to the flaws without naming a CVE, and Unit 42 retracted on 2026-10-25 its update that linked an attack on a European government agency to them. As of 2026-10-22 BACS knew of no attack in Switzerland but said federal PHP applications were affected and that it had no complete overview of the update status, and Swiss cantons, universities, Cyon, the Lodur platform and Publica took sites offline as a precaution. →
    2. 02Stolen UpdraftCentral keys install backdoor plugins on WordPress sites; the plugin update alone does not evict them. TeamUpdraft, the vendor of the UpdraftPlus backup plugin, says unauthorised access to teamupdraft.com on 17 and 18 October probably exposed its customer database, including UpdraftCentral connection keys, and Patchstack and Nethemba report an attacker using those keys to install backdoor plugins on connected WordPress sites, which Patchstack's telemetry dates from 2026-10-18 15:32 (it gives no time zone), and that create hidden administrators. It is not a flaw in UpdraftPlus: the plugin obeys commands signed with a valid key, a key exists on many sites that never used the service because connecting a teamupdraft.com account ticks the option by default, and the update disconnects only connections made before 13:07 BST (12:07 UTC) on 18 October, so keys created later that day have to be deleted by hand. TeamUpdraft says the attacker used a compromised key to enter its own site, and Nethemba saw the stolen keys used again on 2026-10-21, after 1.26.13 shipped. →
    3. 03Symantec: APT28's hidden-Edge backdoor hit law-enforcement and defence targets; at a shipbuilder it added an SSH tunnel. Symantec reports that APT28, which it tracks as Swallowtail, used the HOOKEDGE backdoor against a law-enforcement agency, a naval shipbuilder and a military aircraft-engine manufacturer in two NATO member states, none named. A macro-enabled Word document started a script chain that uses a hidden, off-screen Microsoft Edge window as its HTTP client against a public webhook service; at the shipbuilder the operators then pushed an SSH private key for a persistent tunnel, captured NTLM handshakes from the host itself and ran a previously undocumented in-memory loader, in an intrusion that lasted at least two months. Recorded Future had documented HOOKEDGE against government and diplomatic targets in Romania, Spain and Turkiye in August. →
    Active threats, incidents & disclosures 4Trending vulnerabilities 2Updates to prior coverage 152Action items 33

    01Active threats, incidents & disclosures4 items

    NOTABLENATOB2

    APT28 (Swallowtail) used the HOOKEDGE backdoor against a law-enforcement agency, a naval shipbuilder and an aircraft-engine maker in two NATO states; at the shipbuilder it then pushed an SSH key for a persistent tunnel, captured NTLM handshakes and ran a reflective loader

    Symantec's Threat Hunter Team reports that Swallowtail, which it equates with APT28, Fancy Bear and Forest Blizzard, targeted organizations in two NATO member states: a law-enforcement agency, a naval shipbuilder and a manufacturer of military aircraft engines, none named (Symantec, 2026-10-20). Recorded Future had documented the HOOKEDGE backdoor in August, from campaigns between late September 2025 and early April 2026 against government and diplomatic organizations in Romania, Spain and Turkiye, and assesses with moderate confidence that BlueDelta, which overlaps APT28, is behind it (Recorded Future, 2026-08-27).

    At the shipbuilder the vector was a macro-enabled Word document opened from the Outlook attachment cache on 2026-07-24, so it arrived by email; its macro dropped a VBScript launcher, an HTML pair that auto-submits a hidden form to a webhook endpoint and a batch script (Symantec, 2026-10-20). The batch script kills any running Microsoft Edge and restarts it as a one-pixel, off-screen window carrying an inline script instead of a URL, so the browser fetches the next task from the public webhook.site service, and the batch script then runs the task and uploads its output to the same endpoint (Symantec, 2026-10-20). A second stage then took its tasks from a dedicated address, and seven tasks arrived in about 30 seconds, a cadence Symantec reads as interactive (Symantec, 2026-10-20).

    The operators then pushed an SSH private key onto the host and at once opened an outbound SSH session to their own server with host-key checking disabled, legacy RSA algorithms enabled and a background remote port forward, a persistent route into the internal network; separately a short PowerShell script ran an HTTP listener on loopback and net use against it, forcing the host to authenticate to itself so the NTLM handshake could be captured (Symantec, 2026-10-20). On 2026-07-31 they ran a previously undocumented reflective loader, which decrypts an encrypted file in memory; the payload was not recovered, and the launcher was last seen on 2026-09-24 (Symantec, 2026-10-20).

    Exposure
    Windows endpoints where users can open Office documents from mail and macros from internet-originated documents are allowed, and hosts with outbound access to webhook services. Symantec names no victim or country.
    Detection
    Process-creation telemetry with parent lineage for Word spawning script hosts or the command shell, then msedge.exe killed and restarted with a one-pixel window, an off-screen position and a data: URL argument; network and proxy telemetry for msedge.exe or curl reaching a webhook service; ssh.exe started with an identity file, a remote forward and host-key checking disabled; a PowerShell HTTP listener on loopback followed by net use to localhost; and scheduled-task creation by the launcher or installer, since Symantec says the second-stage installer has a scheduled task and Recorded Future advises detecting scheduled-task abuse (Recorded Future, 2026-08-27) (Symantec, 2026-10-20).

    Triage: the distinctive sequence is an Office document's script host, then Edge killed and relaunched as a one-pixel off-screen window carrying an inline script instead of a URL; the Edge launch alone is not the signal.

    Defender takeaway
    Block macro execution from internet-originated documents and, on any host where a HOOKEDGE launcher is found, hunt for the credential theft and the SSH tunnel described above and reset the credentials the host used, because the shipbuilder intrusion continued long after the dropper ran (Symantec, 2026-10-20).

    Sources: Symantec Threat Hunter Team · Recorded Future Insikt Group
    NOTABLECVE-2020-3259 +4exploitedNATOA2

    AA26-293A: FBI, CISA and German and UK law enforcement describe SafePay ransomware, which enters through VPN credentials and five edge-device and backup-server flaws and factory-resets backup NAS devices before encrypting

    CISA, the FBI, the US Secret Service, Germany's Landeskriminalamt Rheinland-Pfalz and UK law enforcement published advisory AA26-293A on 2026-10-20, drawing on investigations as recently as August 2026; they say SafePay has affected businesses and critical infrastructure in North America, South America, Europe and Australia since August 2024, was among the most active ransomware groups in 2026 and has impacted at least 600 separate victims (CISA et al., 2026-10-20). Initial access is exposed credentials, brute force password attacks and VPN access, with the actors using Mullvad VPN addresses and phished account profiles to reach victim networks and exploit CVE-2020-3259 and CVE-2023-20269 on edge devices, and automated scripts exploiting CVE-2023-27532, CVE-2024-40711 and CVE-2024-40766 on internet-exposed systems (CISA et al., 2026-10-20). CISA's catalog names them as Cisco ASA and Firepower Threat Defense (the first two), Veeam Backup & Replication (the next two) and SonicWall SonicOS flaws (CISA KEV catalog, 2026-10-20).

    Once inside, the actors take administrative rights on existing accounts or create new ones, run commands remotely with PsExec and WinRM, enumerate network shares with ShareFinder.ps1 and SharpShares.exe, disable security software, bypass User Account Control, then move over RDP and remote-management tools (CISA et al., 2026-10-20). They compress data with 7-Zip or WinRAR and exfiltrate it with WinSCP, RClone, FileZilla or OneDrive, and sometimes install AnyDesk or TeamViewer (CISA et al., 2026-10-20). Before encrypting they halt the Volume Shadow Copy Service and reset network-attached storage that holds backup data to factory settings; encryption then appends .SafePay (CISA et al., 2026-10-20).

    Exposure
    An internet-facing Cisco ASA or Firepower Threat Defense, Veeam Backup & Replication or SonicWall SonicOS system that one of the five flaws affects, and any VPN or remote-access account protected by a password alone; the advisory states neither affected versions nor fixes, so they come from each vendor's advisory for the CVE. Cisco calls CVE-2020-3259 reachable by an unauthenticated, remote attacker and SonicWall's CVSS vector for CVE-2024-40766 needs no privileges and no user interaction (Cisco, 2024-02-21; SonicWall, 2024-11-20). CISA's catalog says CVE-2020-3259 affects only specific AnyConnect and WebVPN configurations, and describes CVE-2023-27532 as a Cloud Connect flaw for an unauthenticated user within the backup infrastructure network perimeter (CISA KEV catalog, 2026-10-20).
    Detection
    VPN and firewall authentication logs for repeated failures then a success, Mullvad exit addresses and new accounts; directory audit logs for administrator-group changes; process-creation events for PsExec, WinRM and remote-management tools installed outside a change window; archive and file-transfer tools run by non-administrative workflows; and backup and NAS audit logs for a shadow-copy service stop or a factory reset.

    Triage: 7-Zip, WinRAR, RClone, FileZilla, AnyDesk, TeamViewer, PsExec and WinRM are everyday administrative tools, and the advisory warns against calling them malicious without evidence of actor use (CISA et al., 2026-10-20); a shadow-copy service stop or a factory reset of backup storage that follows them is the discriminating step.

    Defender takeaway
    Confirm that every Cisco ASA or Firepower Threat Defense, Veeam Backup & Replication and SonicWall SonicOS system has the vendor's fix or mitigation for the five flaws, require multi-factor authentication on VPN and administrator accounts, and keep offline backups that are encrypted and immutable so that a factory reset of the NAS does not remove the restore path; the advisory recommends offline, encrypted and immutable backups (CISA et al., 2026-10-20).

    Builds on: Inside IT: SafePay published about 30 GB from Manno, social-assistance files with AHV numbers… · SonicWall CVE-2024-40766: why patched firewalls keep falling to Akira and Fog

    threat21 Oct 03:42Zsingle-source · national CERTOpen finding →
    Sources: CISA, FBI, USSS, LKA Rheinland-Pfalz, UK NCA (AA26-293A) · CISA Known Exploited Vulnerabilities catalog · Cisco (advisory cisco-sa-asaftd-info-disclose, last updated 2024-02-21) · SonicWall (advisory SNWLID-2024-0015, last updated 2024-11-20)
    HIGHupdatedNATOB1

    TeamUpdraft breach: stolen UpdraftCentral connection keys are used to push backdoor plugins to connected WordPress sites, with no flaw in UpdraftPlus and no CVE; deleting the keys is the fix

    TeamUpdraft says unauthorised access to teamupdraft.com on 17 and 18 October and that it believes its customer database was accessed, which holds connection keys for UpdraftCentral, UpdraftVault and UpdraftClone; it says UpdraftPlus plugin files and downloads were not affected, that the attacker used a compromised key to access its site, and that there is no evidence the attacker still has access, and it suspects, from exploit speed and faulty first plugins, that AI accelerated the attack (TeamUpdraft, 2026-10-19). Patchstack reports that an attacker holding valid UpdraftCentral dashboard keys has been installing backdoor plugins on connected WordPress sites since 2026-10-18 at 15:32 (the page gives no time zone), and that this is not a vulnerability in UpdraftPlus: the UpdraftCentral client ships inside every copy of the plugin and executes any command signed with the dashboard's private key once the site holds a key (Patchstack, 2026-10-19; BleepingComputer, 2026-10-20).

    The attacker sends a signed plugin.upload_plugin command as an ordinary POST to the site root and WordPress installs and activates the plugin; each delivered plugin wakes on a secret URL parameter carrying a 32-character token, creates an account with a hardcoded password, gives it the Editor role and then the manage_options and install_plugins capabilities, and logs the visitor in with a cookie, which bypasses the login form, two-factor authentication and login-protection plugins (Patchstack, 2026-10-19). The account stays after the plugin is removed, and early samples crash the site with an HTTP 500 and must not be repaired, because fixing the error switches the backdoor on (Patchstack, 2026-10-19). Nethemba adds a variant that logs the attacker in as an existing administrator without a database trace (Nethemba, 2026-10-19).

    A site holds a key if it was connected to UpdraftCentral or if a teamupdraft.com account was logged in from the plugin, where the option to add the site to UpdraftCentral is ticked by default, and keys do not expire (Patchstack, 2026-10-19); on UpdraftPlus 1.26.4 or 2.26.4 and earlier an attacker needs no key at all, through CVE-2026-10795, fixed in 1.26.5 and 2.26.5 (Patchstack, 2026-10-19). BleepingComputer reports an estimate of up to about 400,000 sites (BleepingComputer, 2026-10-20; Nethemba, 2026-10-19). TeamUpdraft says UpdraftPlus 1.26.13 and 2.26.13 disconnect every UpdraftCentral connection made before 13:07 BST (12:07 UTC) on 18 October; Nethemba says 1.26.12 and 2.26.12 deleted none and that 1.26.13 does not delete keys created on 18 October between 12:07 and 21:00 UTC, which have to be deleted by hand (TeamUpdraft, 2026-10-19; Nethemba, 2026-10-19). Nethemba saw the attacker send UpdraftCentral commands to two of its sites on the morning of 2026-10-21, after 1.26.13 had shipped, blocked by Nethemba's own blocking plugin, with the keys already deleted, and says sites that neither run 1.26.13 or 2.26.13 nor have deleted the key by hand remain at risk (Nethemba, 2026-10-19).

    Exposure
    A WordPress site running UpdraftPlus, free or premium, that holds an UpdraftCentral key; wp option get updraft_central_localkeys prints it, including on sites whose owners never used UpdraftCentral (Patchstack, 2026-10-19). Self-hosted UpdraftCentral is not affected (TeamUpdraft, 2026-10-19).
    Detection
    Web server access logs for POST requests to the site root carrying an UpdraftCentral command whose user agent lacks the library version and URL that genuine dashboard traffic carries, and for GET requests with a short underscore-prefixed parameter and a 32-character token, which mean a backdoor was used; the UpdraftCentral log on the site for plugin.upload_plugin and plugin.delete_plugin commands without a matching administrator action; the user table for an Editor that also holds manage_options (Patchstack, 2026-10-19; Nethemba, 2026-10-19); and the plugin and must-use plugin directories (TeamUpdraft, 2026-10-19). TeamUpdraft asks users to look at the sites listed under their UpdraftCentral account for any they did not knowingly connect since 2026-10-19 and says it is seeing a pattern of unexpected Editor roles (TeamUpdraft, 2026-10-19). The backdoor account stays after the plugin is removed (Patchstack, 2026-10-19).
    Defender takeaway
    Delete the UpdraftCentral key on every site first, then update; if a backdoor is found, delete the key before removing it or the attacker pushes a replacement, and treat the site as compromised (Patchstack, 2026-10-19). IP blocking is not a control, since Patchstack saw the source addresses rotate within a day (Patchstack, 2026-10-19). On UpdraftPlus Premium sites Nethemba reads the code as leaving the teamupdraft.com account password stored in the database, and so in backups, until add-ons are claimed with a token, although the vendor says it is removed after the first connection, a reader found it on all three of their premium sites and in backups going back to 2022, and Nethemba has not found the samples it analysed reading it; changing that account password is therefore part of the cleanup (Nethemba, 2026-10-19).
    Update24 Oct 2026 03:42 UTCrun 2026-10-24T0255Z-intelactionsbodyevidencesourcessourcing_notesummary

    TeamUpdraft's updates of 2026-10-21 and 2026-10-22 say the attacker used a compromised key to access its site, that there is no evidence the attacker still has access, and that the speed of the exploits and the initially faulty backdoor plugins suggest AI was used to accelerate the attack, which is the vendor's inference and comes with no technical evidence (TeamUpdraft, 2026-10-19). It asks users to look at the sites listed under their UpdraftCentral account for any they did not knowingly connect since 2026-10-19, says it is seeing a pattern of unexpected Editor roles, and stresses that UpdraftPlus Premium users should check for unexpected plugins and new administrators even if they never used UpdraftCentral (TeamUpdraft, 2026-10-19).

    Nethemba reports that the attacker kept going after the fix: on 2026-10-20 it tried the secret triggers of already removed backdoors on at least six sites of four administrators, and on the morning of 2026-10-21 it sent UpdraftCentral commands to two of Nethemba's sites after 1.26.13 had been released, blocked by Nethemba's own blocking plugin, with the keys no longer existing (Nethemba, 2026-10-19). A site that has a stolen key and neither updated nor deleted it by hand therefore stays exposed, and a removed backdoor is still probed. Nethemba also reads the code of UpdraftPlus Premium as storing the teamupdraft.com account password in the site database as entered and deleting it only after add-ons are claimed with a token, so it can stay in the database and in backups; a reader found it on all three of their premium sites and in backups going back to 2022, and Nethemba on one of its three, while the vendor says it is removed after the first connection and Nethemba has not found the samples it analysed reading it (Nethemba, 2026-10-19).

    Improvement25 Oct 2026 14:01 UTCrun 2026-10-25T1308Z-auditbody

    TeamUpdraft bases its suspicion that AI accelerated the attack on the speed of the exploits after the breach and the initially faulty backdoor plugins, and publishes no technical evidence beyond that; its own guidance includes checking the plugins list, the Must-Use tab included, for anything not recognised (TeamUpdraft, 2026-10-19).

    Builds on: CVE-2026-10795, UpdraftPlus WordPress backup plugin: unauthenticated authentication bypass to…

    Sources: TeamUpdraft (post updated 2026-10-23) · Patchstack · Nethemba (living post, updated to 2026-10-23) · BleepingComputer
    NOTABLECVE-2026-91403 +2updatedNATOA1

    eMudhra: the private keys of eight intermediate CA certificates were trivially recoverable, so anyone could forge trusted HTTPS certificates; browsers block them, clients with their own trust store or no revocation checking need an update (CVE-2026-111943 in Go)

    On 2026-10-17 a researcher reported to the Chrome Root Program, and Mozilla received a report the same day, that the private keys of eight eMudhra subordinate CA certificates were compromised: the keys were generated with insufficient randomness, which lets anyone forge trusted HTTPS certificates to impersonate any website against clients that have not removed trust in those CAs (Google, 2026-10-20; Mozilla, 2026-10-20). BleepingComputer, citing Mozilla's incident report, says all eight were P-384 keys generated in April 2025 with values below 256, so testing 256 numbers recovers a key, and that eMudhra blames a key-generation script error and found no evidence of end-user certificates (BleepingComputer, 2026-10-20). Google reports that no certificates chaining to the six TLS intermediates were logged to Certificate Transparency before it blocked them, so CT-enforcing clients such as Chrome could not have been attacked (Google, 2026-10-20); the tracker adds that many clients outside browsers do not enforce CT and that it is not known whether attackers used the flaw against them (Dennis Jackson, 2026-10-20).

    Chrome blocked the intermediates on 2026-10-17, Firefox through OneCRL the same day, Microsoft platforms on 2026-10-20 and Apple devices on 2026-10-22, and eMudhra revoked all eight (Dennis Jackson, 2026-10-20; BleepingComputer, 2026-10-20). Google removed the root that signed them, emSign ECC Root CA - G3, from the Chrome Root Store, with the removal expected to reach devices on 2026-10-27, after which some legitimate certificates chaining to it will stop working in Chrome (Google, 2026-10-20); Mozilla removes the signing root from NSS 3.131.1 to protect applications that use its trust store without OneCRL (Mozilla, 2026-10-20). Go 1.27.3 and 1.26.10 filter chains that contain the intermediates in crypto/x509, tracked as CVE-2026-111943 (Go team, 2026-10-20).

    Standalone OpenJDK runtimes, unlike operating-system packages, carry their own keystore (OpenJDK, 2026-10-24). Oracle's Security Alert of 2026-10-21 for CVE-2026-91403 (CVSS 3.1 9.1, component JSSE, remotely exploitable without authentication through the component's APIs, for example a web service that supplies data to them) provided Java updates that distrust the impacted eMudhra intermediate CA certificates (Oracle, 2026-10-21), and a second Security Alert of 2026-10-24, CVE-2026-114820 (CVSS 3.1 9.1, same component and exposure), provides updates that remove the impacted root CA certificates and lists Oracle Java SE 8u511-b32, 11.0.33.0.2, 17.0.21.0.2, 21.0.13.0.2, 25.0.5.0.2 and 27.0.1.0.2 as affected (Oracle, 2026-10-24). The OpenJDK advisory of 2026-10-24 lists 27.0.1.0.2, 25.0.5.0.2, 21.0.13.0.2, 17.0.21.0.2, 11.0.32.1 and 8u504 and earlier as affected (OpenJDK, 2026-10-24), and OpenJDK's guidance tells users of a standalone runtime to act immediately even if they updated in the past few days, preferably by installing an update whose release note names JDK-8395690 and CVE-2026-114820 (OpenJDK, 2026-10-24). If no update is available, OpenJDK gives two alternatives: delete the aliases emsignrootcag1, emsignrootcag2, emsigneccrootcag3 and comodoaaaca from the runtime's cacerts keystore and restart it, or enable revocation checking with -Dcom.sun.net.ssl.checkRevocation=true and -Dcom.sun.security.enableCRLDP=true for TLS clients and use jarsigner -verify -revcheck -strict for signed JARs on JDK 17 and later; the step is repeated for every installed runtime (OpenJDK, 2026-10-24). OpenJDK packages from Arch Linux, Fedora, Gentoo, Oracle Linux and Red Hat Enterprise Linux most likely use the operating system's certificates, so updating those is likely sufficient; Debian and its derivatives, Devuan and Ubuntu among them, need a manual workaround because updating ca-certificates and ca-certificates-java does not refresh the keystore (as superuser, remove /etc/ssl/certs/java/cacerts and run update-ca-certificates --fresh), and the FreeBSD and Homebrew packages need an updated package (OpenJDK, 2026-10-24). NCSC.ch published an advisory on 2026-10-22 and lists the exploitation status as unknown (NCSC.ch, 2026-10-22).

    Exposure
    Any software that keeps its own CA bundle or does not check revocation keeps trusting the intermediates until updated; the tracker lists fixed versions for Python (3.15.1, 3.14.9 and 3.13.17, and pip 26.2.2), Python certifi, rustls-webpki, the curl CA bundle, the Composer ca-bundle, Erlang certifi and several distributions' ca-certificates packages, shows Node.js, Bun and FreeBSD as fixed, and lists Bottlerocket, Godot, LibreOffice and RHEL 7 as still pending (Dennis Jackson, 2026-10-20). The tracker adds that macOS blocks the intermediates but its built-in curl reads /etc/ssl/cert.pem without a revocation check and the file was not updated (Dennis Jackson, 2026-10-20). A client's state is read with the tracker's test hosts: a certificate error means the client does not trust the intermediates (Dennis Jackson, 2026-10-20).
    Detection
    EMudhra says it found no evidence that the intermediates were used to issue end-user certificates (BleepingComputer, 2026-10-20), so a server certificate chain seen in TLS session metadata, proxy or EDR network telemetry that contains one of the eight intermediates or their Sectigo cross-signs, which the tracker lists, is worth treating as a possible forged certificate (Dennis Jackson, 2026-10-20). Google reports that no certificates chaining to the six TLS intermediates were logged to Certificate Transparency before it blocked them, so CT monitoring will not show one (Google, 2026-10-20).

    Triage: a site that still chains to emSign ECC Root CA - G3 failing in Chrome after 2026-10-27 is the announced root removal, not an attack (Google, 2026-10-20).

    Defender takeaway
    Update trust bundles and toolchains on every non-browser client, rebuild container images that embed an old bundle, and do not rely on revocation or CT for those clients; website operators with certificates chaining to emSign ECC Root CA - G3 must install a certificate from another hierarchy before 2026-10-27 (Google, 2026-10-20). A Java runtime already updated on 2026-10-21 needs the update of 2026-10-24 as well (OpenJDK, 2026-10-24), and an ACME client will not replace a certificate under that root outside its renewal window unless it supports ACME Renewal Information, so trigger the renewal by hand (The Hacker News, 2026-10-22).
    Update23 Oct 2026 03:43 UTCrun 2026-10-23T0256Z-intelheadlinesummaryaffected_productscvessourcesevidenceactionsbodyentities

    On 2026-10-21 Oracle issued Security Alert CVE-2026-91403 and the OpenJDK project an advisory for the same event, which reaches Java runtimes as well; Oracle says it also applies to sandboxed Java Web Start and applet clients (Oracle, 2026-10-21). Affected are Oracle Java SE 8u511, 8u511-perf, 11.0.33, 17.0.21, 21.0.13, 25.0.5 and 27.0.1, GraalVM for JDK 17.0.21 and 21.0.13 and GraalVM Enterprise Edition 21.3.20, and OpenJDK lists 27.0.1, 25.0.5, 21.0.13, 17.0.21, 11.0.32.1 and 8u504 and earlier (Oracle, 2026-10-21; OpenJDK, 2026-10-21). Compare the full build string, not the release number, against Oracle's affected list. NCSC.ch's advisory of 2026-10-22 lists the exploitation status as unknown and the OpenJDK guidance as the temporary mitigation (NCSC.ch, 2026-10-22).

    Update25 Oct 2026 03:36 UTCrun 2026-10-25T0256Z-intelheadlinesummarycvesaffected_productsentitiessourcesevidenceactionsbody

    Oracle's second Security Alert of 2026-10-24, CVE-2026-114820 (CVSS 3.1 9.1, component JSSE, remotely exploitable without authentication through the component's APIs), provides Java updates that remove the impacted root CA certificates related to eMudhra (Oracle, 2026-10-24). Its affected list names Oracle Java SE 8u511-b32, 11.0.33.0.2, 17.0.21.0.2, 21.0.13.0.2, 25.0.5.0.2 and 27.0.1.0.2, and the OpenJDK advisory of the same day lists 27.0.1.0.2, 25.0.5.0.2, 21.0.13.0.2, 17.0.21.0.2, 11.0.32.1 and 8u504 and earlier as affected (Oracle, 2026-10-24; OpenJDK, 2026-10-24). OpenJDK tells users of a standalone runtime to act immediately even if they updated in the past few days, by installing an update whose release note names JDK-8395690 and CVE-2026-114820 or, failing that, by deleting the cacerts aliases emsignrootcag1, emsignrootcag2, emsigneccrootcag3 and comodoaaaca or enabling revocation checking (OpenJDK, 2026-10-24). The same page now says Debian, Devuan and Ubuntu OpenJDK packages need the keystore recreated by hand, because updating ca-certificates and ca-certificates-java does not refresh it, which replaces the earlier statement that updating the operating system's certificates suffices there (OpenJDK, 2026-10-24).

    The Python Software Foundation announced CVE-2026-115557 on 2026-10-24: the ssl module now carries a denylist of known compromised certificates that is rejected whatever the SSLContext policy, so it works without revocation checking (Python Software Foundation, 2026-10-24). ENISA's record scores it 9.4 (CVSS 4.0) and lists CPython before 3.16.0 as affected, and the announcement names no release that carries the denylist (ENISA EUVD, 2026-10-24). The incident tracker lists Python 3.15.1, 3.14.9 and 3.13.17 and pip 26.2.2 as patched, and Christian Heimes stresses in the announcement thread that pip before 26.2.2 keeps trusting the compromised intermediates whatever the system trust store holds, because it always loads its own vendored certifi (Dennis Jackson, 2026-10-20; Python Discourse, 2026-10-20).

    Cisco's informational advisory, version 1.1 of 2026-10-24, lists as impacted with remediation a TLS policy that blocks the known eMudhra issuers on Secure Firewall Management Center, Duo Access Gateway 3.2.0, Duo Network Gateway 3.4.1, Duo Agentic Authorization Connector 1.0.1, ThousandEyes Enterprise Agent (Application-hosting Agent 5.2.2, 2026-10-23), Meraki MV Smart Camera Firmware (2026-11-18), IOS XR 26.4.1 (2026-11-30) and Ultra Cloud Core Subscriber Microservices Infrastructure 2026.04.1 (November 2026), keeps further products under investigation, and says Cisco PSIRT is not aware of malicious use (Cisco PSIRT, 2026-10-24).

    eMudhra's board decided on 2026-10-22 to leave the global SSL/TLS certificate business, and its G1 RSA roots keep serving customers during the transition (eMudhra, 2026-10-22). The Hacker News relays Chrome's count of about 30,800 unexpired certificates under the emSign ECC Root CA - G3, which Chrome stops trusting on 2026-10-27, as of 2026-10-21, 99.9 percent of them issued through InCommon, and relays Chrome's point that standard ACME clients do not replace a certificate outside its renewal window unless they support ACME Renewal Information or someone steps in by hand (The Hacker News, 2026-10-22).

    02Trending vulnerabilities2 items

    CRITICALCVE-2026-109618 +1exploitedupdatedNATOA1

    CVE-2026-109618 / CVE-2026-109619, PHP (PHP2Shell): two unauthenticated remote-code-execution flaws in the runtime, fixed on 2026-10-21 in 8.5.12, 8.4.27, 8.3.36 and 8.2.35, with attack traffic already observed and no upstream release for 8.1 and older (CVSS 4.0 9.2)

    The PHP project released PHP 8.5.12, 8.4.27, 8.3.36 and 8.2.35 on 2026-10-21 to fix two critical vulnerabilities that can allow unauthenticated remote code execution, CVE-2026-109618 and CVE-2026-109619, both CVSS 4.0 9.2, and urges all PHP users to update immediately (PHP, 2026-10-21; PHP, 2026-10-20). CVE-2026-109618 is memory corruption when PHP handles an out-of-memory error: it aborts the request with longjmp() while internal request data is only partly initialised, and request cleanup then treats the invalid data as valid (PHP, 2026-10-21). CVE-2026-109619 is stale global state: request-owned state survives a fatal error after its memory is freed, and a later request on the same worker uses it (PHP, 2026-10-21). Every PHP installation serving web requests is at risk, including PHP-FPM, Apache with mod_php, php-cgi, FrankenPHP and LiteSpeed; the project rates mod_php highest, with a demonstrated exploit where an unauthenticated request can trigger the attack before any application code runs (PHP, 2026-10-21). The confirmed impact is unauthenticated code execution as the web worker account, validated against mod_php with default settings, and for the second flaw against both mod_php and FPM through session upload progress, which is on by default and needs no session_start() call (PHP, 2026-10-21; PHP, 2026-10-21). The advisory for the second flaw also lists heap use-after-free and memory disclosure among the confirmed impacts (PHP, 2026-10-21), and Sansec says the leaked worker memory can hold passwords, session tokens, API keys and TLS private keys from other requests, and files the upload-progress memory disclosure under CVE-2026-109618 where the PHP advisories list it under CVE-2026-109619 (Sansec, 2026-10-21).

    The fixed branches are 8.2 to 8.5; PHP 8.1 and older no longer receive updates, and the project published minimal source backports for 7.4, 8.0 and 8.1 for people who build from source, while distributions and LTS vendors received the patches in advance (PHP, 2026-10-21). Cloudflare says all web servers running PHP 5.4 or later are affected (Cloudflare, 2026-10-21). The fix is in the runtime, so updating WordPress, a plugin or a theme does not change exposure (Wordfence, 2026-10-20). Products that embed PHP are affected through it: Palo Alto Networks lists PAN-OS 12.2, 12.1, 11.2, 11.1 and 10.2 as affected, with all but one of its hotfixes released and 11.1.7-h12 due on 2026-10-30, and notes that the issue applies to PAN-OS software on PA-Series and VM-Series firewalls and on Panorama (Palo Alto Networks, 2026-10-29), and ENISA, the Commission, CERT-EU, Europol EC3 and the CSIRTs network point to NCSC-NL's repository for software that bundles PHP (ENISA, 2026-10-21).

    CISA added both flaws to its Known Exploited Vulnerabilities catalog on 2026-10-26 and revised its alert the same day; the alert names no actor and does not say how CISA learned of the exploitation (CISA, 2026-10-26; CISA KEV, 2026-10-26). The first documented code execution is a honeypot report by Adel Karimi of Perplexity dated 2026-10-24: on three disposable targets running PHP 8.4.26 under single-worker Apache mod_php, 35 execution-stage requests were each followed by a shell started directly by an Apache worker, in a four-stage multipart chain (memory pressure, memory disclosure, more memory pressure, an execution request adjusted to the leaked layout); the chain is consistent with CVE-2026-109618, the records do not establish a CVE-2026-109619 path and no second-stage download was observed (Karimi, 2026-10-24; SecurityWeek, 2026-10-26; BleepingComputer, 2026-10-26). Earlier reports describe scanning and attempts: Cloudflare saw attack traffic against its customers while the flaws were still leaked (Cloudflare, 2026-10-21), watchTowr's honeypots recorded exploitation on 2026-10-21 (watchTowr, 2026-10-21), and VulnCheck's canary hosts recorded scanning from 08:37 UTC that day (VulnCheck, 2026-10-21). VulnCheck says it holds a working exploit for CVE-2026-109618 that, as of 2026-10-26, supports mod_php on PHP 8.2.28 to 8.2.34, 8.3.22 to 8.3.35, 8.4.10 to 8.4.26 and 8.5.10 to 8.5.11 with default settings and display_errors off, and WordPress on default Docker installs (VulnCheck, 2026-10-26). Palo Alto Networks' advisory for both flaws now states that active exploitation has been observed targeting its management interface exposed to the internet, after a copy of 2026-10-27 said very limited exploitation, and names no victim or actor (Palo Alto Networks, 2026-10-28; Palo Alto Networks, 2026-10-27); Dream Research's recovered artifacts, described next, add a detailed account of a production compromise through these CVEs, while Unit 42 retracted on 2026-10-25 its update of 2026-10-23 that had linked an attack on a European government agency to these flaws and says it is unable to confirm that the initial access is associated with them (Unit 42, 2026-10-25), and CrowdStrike, Wordfence and Sophos reported no successful exploitation (CrowdStrike, 2026-10-23; Wordfence, 2026-10-22; Sophos, 2026-10-22). Dream Research describes the chain it recovered: a custom PHP2Shell build tailored to the management web server of one unnamed vendor's firewall software (PHP 8.0.30), combined with the Linux kernel flaw CVE-2026-46300, gave unauthenticated root on 24 internet-facing firewalls and led, through the second of two firewalls of a US government-affiliated organization (the first crashed), to an outbound SSH tunnel, the rooting of the organization's firewall management server and a password-spraying attack on its Active Directory, and CISA's entry for the kernel flaw says the same chaining (Dream Research, 2026-10-27; CISA KEV, 2026-10-28). In Switzerland, SRF reported on 2026-10-26 that TCS Camping is warning about 15,000 customers in connection with the PHP flaw after a data theft at its booking-system supplier Camping.Care, and Blick, citing SRF, writes that the attackers used the two flaws; neither names a CVE (SRF, 2026-10-26; Blick, 2026-10-26).

    PHP posted an informational advisory on 2026-10-23 for a third code-execution path, CVE-2026-115560, that the October releases do not fix: it needs an application that sets session.serialize_handler to php_binary or php_serialize, switches it to php with ini_set() before session_start(), and leaves session upload progress enabled; PHP calls it an unlikely vector, issues no emergency release, suggests session.upload_progress.enabled=0 until the next regular bugfix release and will publish the full advisory then (PHP, 2026-10-23). PHP has not stated affected versions or a severity score for CVE-2026-115560, and no exploitation is reported (The Hacker News, 2026-10-23).

    Assessments of how easy exploitation is diverge. Sansec says an attack needs specific knowledge of the target server and specific conditions on it, that nobody has shown a single request that works against any vulnerable server, as there was for Log4Shell and Shellshock, that a researcher who tested the public proofs of concept found they do not work against a default PHP installation, and that mass exploitation is therefore less likely than it first expected, while it advises updating anyway (Sansec, 2026-10-21). CrowdStrike expects no single universal exploit but says a reliable one is feasible against a specific deployment profile, such as a fixed Docker image, because an attacker can calibrate offsets and heap grooming in advance (CrowdStrike, 2026-10-23), and the SANS Internet Storm Center, reading the public proofs of concept, finds a universal, reliable one-click exploit quite difficult (SANS ISC, 2026-10-23). Red Hat rates CVE-2026-109618 Important (CVSS 3.1 8.1 with high attack complexity, against 9.8 at NVD), says reliable exploitation requires precise heap manipulation and timing, and says its standard Red Hat Enterprise Linux deployments use PHP-FPM, whose workers exit on early initialization failures, so code execution is difficult in practice and typically needs non-default behavior such as parsing a second request body during script execution (Red Hat, 2026-10-21). That sits against VulnCheck's weaponized exploit above, and the advice of the PHP project, BACS, CERT-EU and ENISA to update is unchanged. Vendors of products that embed PHP report as follows: Citrix lists the NetScaler management GUI on every supported version (NetScaler Product Security, 2026-10-22); Cisco lists Expressway, Ultra-Reliable Wireless Backhaul, Duo Access Gateway and ISE Portal Builder, with fixes at 15.5.21, 27.1.1 (November 2026) and 3.2.0 (Cisco PSIRT, 2026-10-28); Fortinet keeps only FortiDDoS, FortiSOAR and FortiSOAR PaaS under investigation (Fortinet PSIRT, 2026-10-26); Splunk lists its products as unaffected or remedied (Splunk, 2026-10-26); Tenable's patch SC-202610.2 updates the bundled PHP in Security Center and Enclave Security to 8.2.35 (Tenable, 2026-10-23; Tenable, 2026-10-23); Siemens lists RUGGEDCOM APE1808 devices running PAN-OS as affected (Siemens ProductCERT, 2026-10-23); and WatchGuard says none of its products are affected (WatchGuard PSIRT, 2026-10-21).

    Exposure
    Every internet-facing server or container image that runs PHP, and every product, appliance or administration interface that embeds it, internal ones included; BACS stresses that operators often do not know a product uses PHP, and that distribution packages can carry the fix under an unchanged version number, so the running version and the vendor's security information decide (BACS, 2026-10-21). On WordPress the running PHP version is shown under Tools > Site Health > Info > Server (Wordfence, 2026-10-20).
    Detection
    AWS, as relayed by Inside IT, advises an operator who cannot exclude a compromise to start the investigation at least from 2026-10-17, the date of its earliest honeypot exploit attempt (Inside IT, 2026-10-22); the telemetry is, in web access logs, multipart POST requests to PHP endpoints from unfamiliar sources, including multipart requests that use the upload-progress code path, which VulnCheck's canaries saw scanners send, and an attempt to drop a web shell through a server-side session file, which one canary saw fail (VulnCheck, 2026-10-21); in PHP error logs and process telemetry, worker crashes and fatal errors, and processes started by the web server or PHP worker account, since the code runs with the web worker's privileges (PHP, 2026-10-21), above all a shell started directly by an Apache worker, as in Karimi's honeypots, which CISA also names as the behaviour to watch for (Karimi, 2026-10-24; CISA, 2026-10-26); and in the web root, new or changed files. BACS asks operators to examine logs and systems for unusual requests, crashes, changed files and unexpected processes, and says an update does not remove a compromise that has already happened (BACS, 2026-10-21). ReliaQuest, which reproduced exploitation of CVE-2026-109618 in controlled labs, reports a sequence across the PHP error, Apache access, Apache error and system logs: a POST that returns HTTP 500, PHP memory exhaustion reported "in Unknown on line 0" where an ordinary memory error names the application file and line, heap corruption, and an abort of the Apache worker that handled the request within seconds; each event can be benign alone, the full sequence on the same process is the stronger indication, and linking request to crash needs the process ID in the Apache access log as well as the error log (ReliaQuest, 2026-10-22).

    Triage: an unexplained outage of a public-sector, university or fire-service website on 2026-10-21 or 2026-10-22 is more likely a precautionary take-down than a denial-of-service attack where the operator says so, as Basel-Stadt, Publica and the Lodur provider each did; the discriminators are the operator's statement and restoration after the update (Canton of Basel-Stadt, 2026-10-21; Publica, 2026-10-21; nau.ch, 2026-10-21).

    Defender takeaway
    Update the PHP runtime on every internet-facing host today and restart the workers, internet-facing systems first and then internal and embedded installations, and take the management interface of every appliance that embeds PHP off the internet; where an update must wait, CERT-EU advises isolating internet-facing applications from public networks until the fix is applied and verified (CERT-EU, 2026-10-21). The project's interim settings (disable session upload progress, enforce memory_limit=-1 administratively, a low post_max_size, block multipart requests at the proxy where uploads are not needed) reduce only some attack paths, and BACS says a web application firewall alone is not enough (BACS, 2026-10-21; PHP, 2026-10-21). PHP 8.1 and older need a vendor backport, the project's source backports or an upgrade, ASLR is not a safe reason to wait on x86-64 given VulnCheck's exploit, and an update does not remove an attacker who is already in. The October releases do not fix CVE-2026-115560, so keep session upload progress disabled on updated hosts until PHP's next regular release (PHP, 2026-10-23). Because the flaws can disclose worker memory, rotating session secrets, API keys and other credentials that the PHP workers handled is a reasonable step on hosts that were reachable while unpatched.
    Update22 Oct 2026 03:45 UTCrun 2026-10-22T0256Z-intelactionsbodyclassificationconfidencecvesevidenceheadlineimmediate_actionpriorityregionssourcessourcing_notesummarytagstechniquestitle

    The fixed releases shipped on 2026-10-21 between 13:30 and 14:00 UTC, depending on the source, together with the technical advisories, so the earlier statement that no fix existed no longer holds (CERT-EU, 2026-10-21; ENISA, 2026-10-21; Sansec, 2026-10-21). ENISA, the European Commission, CERT-EU, Europol EC3 and the CSIRTs network advise organisations to identify their PHP-based applications, coordinate with hosting and service providers, apply the update to internet-facing systems first, and isolate exposed applications from public networks where that is not possible (ENISA, 2026-10-21). BACS published its warning the same day, telling operators to check embedded and internal PHP installations as well as public sites, to restart long-running PHP processes and rebuild containers, to verify the running version, and, where an immediate update is impossible, to consider disconnecting critical applications from the internet (BACS, 2026-10-21). BACS told RTS it knows of no reliable, confirmed substitute for the updates and that the interim measures give no guaranteed full protection (RTS, 2026-10-21).

    Attack traffic was reported on the day: Cloudflare saw it against its customers while the flaws were still leaked, watchTowr's honeypots recorded exploitation, and VulnCheck's canary hosts logged scanning from 08:37 UTC and at least one unsuccessful web-shell attempt through a session file, while VulnCheck says it holds a working exploit for CVE-2026-109618 that succeeds on x86-64 with ASLR enabled; no source reports a confirmed compromise (Cloudflare, 2026-10-21; watchTowr, 2026-10-21; VulnCheck, 2026-10-21). Palo Alto Networks confirmed that every PAN-OS and Panorama release is affected through the management web interface, with no fixed release yet (Palo Alto Networks, 2026-10-21).

    Public-sector sites went offline as a precaution, mostly on the evening of 2026-10-21. The canton of Basel-Stadt activated its crisis staff and shut down every PHP-run cantonal website with no restoration time given, while stating that no security-relevant incident had been registered (Canton of Basel-Stadt, 2026-10-21). The Ticino cantonal administration temporarily disabled some online services, the website of the University of Geneva was unreachable, and the EPFL site displayed a notice that its pages were currently unreachable, although nau.ch says it still worked (nau.ch, 2026-10-21); RTS lists the EPFL and University of Geneva sites as temporarily suspended (RTS, 2026-10-21). Several Neuchâtel canton and city sites were unreachable without an official statement; Arcinfo presumes a link to the PHP flaw, which the state had not confirmed (RTS, 2026-10-21). Lodur, the platform many Swiss fire brigades use to manage their fire brigades and alarm groups, was out of service because its provider took its servers offline as a precaution: users could neither read stored data nor edit alarm groups, though the Valais cantonal fire office said this does not necessarily mean the alarm-triggering systems are down (nau.ch, 2026-10-21; RTS, 2026-10-21). Publica took its website offline on the afternoon of 2026-10-21 as a precaution, says it has closed the vulnerabilities with security updates and was back online that evening, and states the outage is unrelated to the cyberattack on its software supplier (Publica, 2026-10-21). Dutch authorities took DigiD, MijnOverheid, Overheid.nl, TweedeKamer.nl and the tax authority offline for a time, and most were back by that evening (Security.nl, 2026-10-21); RTS reports that the site of the Paris police prefecture was also suspended (RTS, 2026-10-21). Basel-Stadt said it would report on progress on the morning of 2026-10-22, and no restoration status had been published as of the early morning of 2026-10-22 UTC.

    Update23 Oct 2026 03:45 UTCrun 2026-10-23T0256Z-intelsummarysourcesevidencesourcing_notebody

    BACS told Keystone-SDA and SRF on 2026-10-22 that no attack in Switzerland attributable to the two flaws is known to it, while stressing that unrecognised or unreported incidents cannot be excluded and that it has no reliable figures on affected websites, shops and internal applications (SRF, 2026-10-22; watson.ch, 2026-10-22). It also said that PHP-based applications of the federal administration were affected, that the responsible bodies were told to update, and that it has no complete overview of the update status (watson.ch, 2026-10-22; Inside IT, 2026-10-22).

    The shutdowns were large and mostly short. The Basel host Cyon took all of its more than 100,000 customer websites offline for some hours and says everything has run again since the morning of 2026-10-22; the University of Geneva's site was still unreachable that afternoon, and Basel-Stadt still had pages offline at midday and says it found no data outflow (SRF, 2026-10-22). Neuchâtel disabled all PHP-based sites, ne.ch and many commune pages among them, its IT department installed the fix, and the EPFL gave its members access to its services by VPN while its site stayed down (Inside IT, 2026-10-22). Risikomonitor's live view counts 576,549 checked Swiss web servers, 540,169 already patched and 20,231 (3.5 %) still affected, with the PHP version hidden on the other 16,149 (Inside IT, 2026-10-22).

    In the United Kingdom, a minister told the House of Lords on 2026-10-22 that 32 government services were unavailable and four partly unavailable, largely from precautionary measures while updates and mitigations were applied, and that the government knows of no confirmed compromise of central government systems, an assessment she extended to the wider public sector (UKAuthority, 2026-10-22; The Register, 2026-10-22). The Register adds that government sites in Germany, France, Belgium, Finland and the Netherlands were also taken offline as a precaution and that the NCSC sent 50,000 warnings (The Register, 2026-10-22).

    Attack activity began before the release. Inside IT, citing a post by Cloudflare's chief executive, reports millions of attack attempts, the first at 07:43 UTC on 2026-10-21, more than six hours before the release, and relays that AWS threat intelligence recorded an exploit attempt against a honeypot on 2026-10-17 from a commercial anonymisation network, proofs of concept and attempts by several actors from 2026-10-21, and advises an operator who cannot exclude a compromise to start the investigation at least from 2026-10-17; whether the attempts would have succeeded on real systems is open (Inside IT, 2026-10-22). The Register likewise reports that AWS says attackers were exploiting the flaws four days before disclosure (The Register, 2026-10-22).

    Update24 Oct 2026 03:36 UTCrun 2026-10-24T0255Z-intelactionsbodyevidencesourcessourcing_notesummarytechniques

    Palo Alto Networks' Unit 42 added to its brief on 2026-10-23 a report that linked an attack on a European government agency to these flaws and retracted that update on 2026-10-25; the update of 2026-10-26 below carries the retraction (Unit 42, 2026-10-25).

    Other vendors report no successful exploitation. CrowdStrike has not observed any in the wild or in its honeypots and expects a reliable exploit only against a specific deployment profile, such as a fixed Docker image (CrowdStrike, 2026-10-23); Wordfence knows of no compromise among the more than 74,000 attempts its firewall intercepted (Wordfence, 2026-10-22); and Sophos corrected on 2026-10-22 its earlier report of successful exploitation to scanning and a small number of failed attempts, one of them a blocked attempt to deliver a likely Mirai-derived botnet variant (Sophos, 2026-10-22). VulnCheck's exploit now covers PHP 8.4.10 to 8.4.26 and 8.5.10 and 8.5.11 on AMD64 and ARM64 with default settings and display_errors off under mod_php, and WordPress on default Docker installs, as of 5:30 PM ET on 2026-10-23 (VulnCheck, 2026-10-21). Neither flaw is in CISA's KEV catalog as of version 2026.10.23 (CISA KEV, 2026-10-23).

    PHP posted an informational advisory on 2026-10-23 for a third code-execution path, CVE-2026-115560, that the October releases do not fix: it needs an application that sets session.serialize_handler to php_binary or php_serialize, switches it to php with ini_set() before session_start(), and leaves session upload progress enabled (PHP, 2026-10-23). PHP calls it an unlikely vector, issues no emergency release, suggests session.upload_progress.enabled=0 until the next regular bugfix release, says a host that applied that setting from the 2026-10-21 advisory should keep it, and will publish the full advisory with the next release (PHP, 2026-10-23). PHP has not stated affected versions or a severity score, and no exploitation is reported (The Hacker News, 2026-10-23).

    Vendor statuses moved. Cisco's advisory, revised on 2026-10-23, now describes a workaround for Expressway in its bug record, keeps the fixed release 15.5.21 undated, lists Ultra-Reliable Wireless Backhaul with a fix in 27.1.1 (February 2027), exploitable there only by an authenticated administrator and with a workaround described, and lists Duo Access Gateway with fixed release 3.2.0 (Cisco PSIRT, 2026-10-23). Splunk now lists only Smartlook as under investigation (Splunk, 2026-10-23). Palo Alto Networks' advisory now lists WF-500 and WF-500-B as unaffected, a correction its timeline dates 2026-10-23, its table no longer distinguishes Panorama, and a note says the issue applies to PAN-OS software on PA-Series and VM-Series firewalls and on Panorama; the PAN-OS hotfix dates of 2026-10-26 to 2026-10-30 are unchanged (Palo Alto Networks, 2026-10-24).

    Update25 Oct 2026 03:38 UTCrun 2026-10-25T0256Z-intelaffected_productsentitiessourcesevidencebody

    Tenable's Security Center (versions 6.6.0, 6.7.2, 6.8.0 and 6.9.1) and Enclave Security (1.7.0, 1.8.0 and 1.9.1) bundle PHP, and patch SC-202610.2 updates the bundled PHP to 8.2.35 (Tenable, 2026-10-23; Tenable, 2026-10-23). Siemens lists RUGGEDCOM APE1808 application-hosting devices running Palo Alto Networks PAN-OS 10.2, 11.1, 11.2, 12.1 and 12.2 as affected by the two flaws and gives patch and update information only through customer support (Siemens ProductCERT, 2026-10-23).

    ReliaQuest says it independently reproduced exploitation of CVE-2026-109618 in controlled labs, with code execution only where the exploit's prerequisites were already present, and that it has not observed successful exploitation in customer environments (ReliaQuest, 2026-10-22). The sequence it saw in its logs is a POST that returns HTTP 500, PHP memory exhaustion reported "in Unknown on line 0", heap corruption and an abort of the Apache worker that handled the request within seconds, which it reads as a stronger indication when the events involve the same process (ReliaQuest, 2026-10-22).

    Update26 Oct 2026 03:39 UTCrun 2026-10-26T0256Z-intelbodyevidenceimmediate_actionsourcessourcing_notesummarytechniques

    Palo Alto Networks' Unit 42 retracted on 2026-10-25 the update of 2026-10-23 in which it had reported post-exploitation by agentic attack platforms at a European government entity. Its brief now says that what its researchers saw was an attack against a European government agency executed in the context of a vulnerable PHP application, that this led them to believe it was exploitation of these flaws, and that it is unable to confirm that the initial access is associated with the specific PHP2Shell CVEs (Unit 42, 2026-10-25). The observations of the retracted update, including the command-batch markers and the Active Directory activity, are no longer part of the brief. The brief still recommends assessing exposure and patching immediately (Unit 42, 2026-10-25).

    What remains documented is scanning and attack attempts, reported by Cloudflare, watchTowr, VulnCheck and others, and VulnCheck's working exploit for specific PHP builds; CrowdStrike, Sophos and Wordfence report no successful exploitation, and no source tied a named CVE to a production compromise. The Hacker News article of 2026-10-25 relays the retracted account and carries no correction (The Hacker News, 2026-10-25).

    Update27 Oct 2026 03:48 UTCrun 2026-10-27T0256Z-intelbodycvesevidenceheadlineimmediate_actionsourcessourcing_notesummarytags

    CISA added both flaws to its Known Exploited Vulnerabilities catalog on 2026-10-26 and updated its alert the same day, which still tells operators to patch internet-facing systems first, to take PHP-based systems offline where they cannot be patched or run PHP 8.1 or earlier, and to monitor for PHP worker processes launching shells or downloading tools (CISA, 2026-10-26; CISA KEV, 2026-10-26). The listing follows a honeypot report by Adel Karimi of Perplexity dated 2026-10-24, relayed by SecurityWeek and BleepingComputer on 2026-10-26, that documents the first successful code execution: 35 execution-stage requests against three honeypots running PHP 8.4.26 under single-worker mod_php, each followed by a shell started directly by an Apache worker, consistent with CVE-2026-109618 and not establishing a separate CVE-2026-109619 path (Karimi, 2026-10-24; SecurityWeek, 2026-10-26; BleepingComputer, 2026-10-26). It is honeypot evidence: no second-stage download was observed, Karimi says it does not measure exploit reliability on other worker counts, PHP builds or applications, and, as of 2026-10-27, no source ties a named CVE to a production compromise; Unit 42's retraction of 2026-10-25 stands. Expel's Marcus Hutchins calls it by far the highest-quality confirmation of in-the-wild exploitation he has seen (BleepingComputer, 2026-10-26).

    VulnCheck's page, updated as of 2026-10-26, extends its working exploit to mod_php on PHP 8.2.28 to 8.2.34 and 8.3.22 to 8.3.35 as well as 8.4.10 to 8.4.26 and 8.5.10 to 8.5.11 (VulnCheck, 2026-10-26). In Switzerland, SRF reports on 2026-10-26 that TCS Camping is warning about 15,000 customers because data was stolen at its booking-system supplier Camping.Care in connection with the PHP flaw: names, telephone numbers and reservation numbers were used in fraudulent WhatsApp messages, and TCS says account and IBAN data were not affected; neither SRF nor TCS names a CVE or the entry point (SRF, 2026-10-26).

    Update28 Oct 2026 03:41 UTCrun 2026-10-28T0256Z-intelsummaryimmediate_actionevidencesourcing_notebodysources

    Palo Alto Networks' advisory for both flaws now states that active exploitation has been observed targeting the Palo Alto Networks management interface exposed to the internet, and its severity is Critical with urgency Highest (Palo Alto Networks, 2026-10-28). A copy of the page taken on 2026-10-27 at 17:10 UTC said instead that very limited exploitation had been observed (Palo Alto Networks, 2026-10-27). The advisory names no victim, actor or post-exploitation behaviour and does not say which flaw was used. It is a vendor statement about its own product, Unit 42's retraction of 2026-10-25 stands (Palo Alto Networks Unit 42, 2026-10-25), and Palo Alto's remaining PAN-OS hotfixes are due by 2026-10-30 (Palo Alto Networks, 2026-10-28).

    Update29 Oct 2026 07:03 UTCrun 2026-10-29T0255Z-intelsummaryupdated_attechniquesentitiessourcesevidencesourcing_notereferencesactionsbody

    CISA's catalog entry of 2026-10-28 for the Linux kernel flaw CVE-2026-46300 says it can be chained with CVE-2026-109618 and CVE-2026-109619 to implant PHP webshells and run commands as root (CISA KEV, 2026-10-28). Dream Research reports a chain that takes these flaws to root on appliances: a custom, non-public PHP2Shell build for the management web server of one large vendor's firewall software, which runs PHP 8.0.30 with a high memory limit, gave code execution as the nobody user, and the kernel flaw lifted it to root in one HTTP request in under 15 minutes (Dream Research, 2026-10-27). Dream found the build tuned to eleven firmware versions, nine exploitable, with offsets from the vendor's own PHP library, the deployment-specific calibration CrowdStrike expects a reliable exploit to need (an inference) (Dream Research, 2026-10-27; CrowdStrike, 2026-10-23). From about 19,000 internet-facing firewalls of that vendor it narrowed to 240, rooted 24 and left a PHP webshell on each, and from the second of two firewalls of a US government-affiliated organization (the first crashed) built a persistent outbound SSH tunnel, rooted the organization's firewall management server through it and began an attack on its Active Directory; Dream could not tell whether that attack succeeded, does not name the vendor, calls the campaign apparently Chinese and AI-assisted and does not attribute it to a named actor (Dream Research, 2026-10-27). The targets were firewalls whose management interfaces were reachable from the internet (Dream Research, 2026-10-27).

    Palo Alto Networks' advisory as read on 2026-10-29 lists all PAN-OS hotfixes as released except 11.1.7-h12, due on 2026-10-30 (Palo Alto Networks, 2026-10-29).

    Builds on: CVE-2026-46300 (Fragnesia): CISA lists the Linux kernel root flaw as exploited, chained with…

    NOTABLECVE-2026-87337 +11updatedNATOA2

    Oracle October 2026 Critical Patch Update: six unauthenticated network CVSS 10.0 rows in Access Manager, SOA Suite, E-Business Suite and JD Edwards, and WebLogic CVSS 9.8 flaws reachable over HTTP and T3/IIOP

    Oracle's Critical Patch Update advisory of 2026-10-20 contains 1295 new security patches (Oracle, 2026-10-20). Its risk matrices score 112 rows at CVSS 3.1 9.0 or higher, and 90 of those, covering 73 distinct CVE ids, are marked as remotely exploitable without authentication with a network attack vector (Oracle, 2026-10-20). Six rows score 10.0: CVE-2026-87337 in the Access Manager Authentication Engine (HTTP), CVE-2026-91231 in the SOA Suite Adapters (HTTP), CVE-2026-91216 and CVE-2026-91221 in the SOA Suite Fabric Layer (T3 and IIOP), CVE-2026-101809 in the E-Business Suite Workflow Notification Mailer (HTTP, 12.2.3 to 12.2.15) and CVE-2026-91299 in the JD Edwards EnterpriseOne Orchestrator security component (HTTP, 9.2.0.0 to 9.2.26.5) (Oracle, 2026-10-20). WebLogic Server 12.2.1.4.0, 14.1.1.0.0, 14.1.2.0.0 and 15.1.1.0.0 carry CVE-2026-91323 (HTTP) and CVE-2026-83399 (T3 and IIOP) at 9.8 and CVE-2026-87302 (HTTP) at 9.1; Identity Manager's End User Self Service has CVE-2026-91408 (T3 and IIOP, 9.8) and Access Manager's SAML path CVE-2026-87251 (9.8) (Oracle, 2026-10-20). The advisory states no exploitation for any row and carries only Oracle's standing sentence that it receives reports of attempts to exploit already-patched flaws (Oracle, 2026-10-20); for CVE-2026-101767, Defused's decoys have since recorded exploit attempts, described in the update below. Oracle points to My Oracle Support documents for patch availability (Oracle, 2026-10-20).

    Exposure
    A WebLogic, SOA Suite, Access Manager or Identity Manager server whose HTTP, T3 or IIOP listener, or SAML endpoint, answers from a network that is not the management network; an E-Business Suite 12.2.3 to 12.2.15 web tier with the Workflow Notification Mailer, or whose URL firewall allows the Thin Client Framework servlet, as the default configuration does (Horizon3.ai, 2026-10-20); a JD Edwards EnterpriseOne 9.2 server with the Orchestrator. The product version comes from the product's own version command or the inventory.
    Detection
    Oracle names no indicator. For the E-Business Suite flaw CVE-2026-101767, Horizon3 lists unauthenticated POST requests to the Thin Client Framework servlet followed by backend database activity, SQL reconnaissance shortly after unauthenticated web requests, and the oracle account spawning a shell through the Concurrent Manager (Horizon3.ai, 2026-10-20). Otherwise watch web and application-server access logs and the WebLogic and SOA server logs for requests to the affected components from untrusted sources, T3 and IIOP connections from unexpected networks in flow or load-balancer logs, and Java process-creation events where the middleware process spawns a shell, an interpreter or a download tool.
    Defender takeaway
    Apply the October 2026 update to the six 10.0 components first wherever they are reachable beyond the management network, together with any E-Business Suite web tier exposed to CVE-2026-101767, whose chain Horizon3 has described, then to the WebLogic and Identity Manager rows; where a patch cannot go in at once, remove the Thin Client Framework servlet from the EBS URL firewall allowlist and restrict the EBS web tier to trusted networks (Horizon3.ai, 2026-10-20). Oracle's next scheduled update is the Critical Security Patch Update of 17 November 2026 (Oracle, 2026-10-20).
    Improvement25 Oct 2026 14:09 UTCrun 2026-10-25T1308Z-auditcvestechniquessourcessourcing_notebody

    Horizon3, which reported CVE-2026-101767 to Oracle on 2026-07-24, described the chain on release day, without publishing exploit code. An unauthenticated SQL injection in the Thin Client Framework servlet of E-Business Suite 12.2.3 to 12.2.15 (Application Object Library, Core component, CVSS 3.1 9.8), which the default EBS URL firewall configuration permits, gives SQL execution as the APPS database user and then operating-system commands as the EBS application account (Horizon3.ai, 2026-10-20). Until the update is in, Horizon3 advises removing the servlet from the URL firewall allowlist and restricting the web tier to trusted networks, measures that reduce exposure but do not replace patching (Horizon3.ai, 2026-10-20). Oracle scores the row 9.8, unauthenticated over HTTP (Oracle, 2026-10-20).

    Update29 Oct 2026 04:56 UTCrun 2026-10-29T0255Z-intelheadlinesummaryupdated_atsourcesevidencesourcing_noteactionsbody

    Threat-intelligence firm Defused reported that its Oracle E-Business Suite decoys recorded exploratory exploit attempts against CVE-2026-101767 on the morning of 2026-10-27, one week after the October update (SecurityWeek, 2026-10-28; BleepingComputer, 2026-10-28). BleepingComputer notes that Oracle has not tagged the flaw as exploited and that Shadowserver tracks more than 670 internet-exposed E-Business Suite instances, without saying how many are decoys or already patched (BleepingComputer, 2026-10-28). SecurityWeek adds that there have been no other reports of in-the-wild exploitation; the attempts hit decoys, and neither article describes a compromise of a production system (SecurityWeek, 2026-10-28).

    DirtyDecrypt (DirtyCBC): public root exploit for a Linux kernel RxGK page-cache write, mapped to CVE-2026-31635, on kernels built with CONFIG_RXGK

    First published 2026-05-20 · open finding →

    Correction21 Oct 2026 20:45 UTCrun 2026-10-21T1148Z-auditactionsaffected_productsbodyclassificationconfidencecvesevent_dateevidenceheadlinesectorssourcessourcing_notesummarytechniquestitle

    DirtyDecrypt's link to CVE-2026-31635 comes from Will Dormann and the NVD record's reference to the proof of concept; the kernel's own CVE description is an inverted RESPONSE authenticator length check hitting a BUG_ON, scored 7.5 as a network denial of service, affecting kernels from 6.16 and fixed in 6.18.23, 6.19.13 and 7.0. The proof of concept was public by 2026-05-18, not first published on 2026-05-19, and the exposure follows the CONFIG_RXGK build option and kernel version rather than a list of unaffected distributions.

    DirtyDecrypt's tie to CVE-2026-31635 is an attribution by Will Dormann and the NVD record's link to the proof of concept, not the discoverers' own: the kernel's description of CVE-2026-31635 is an inverted length check that lets oversized RESPONSE authenticators reach rxgk_decrypt_skb() and hit a BUG_ON, scored CVSS 3.1 7.5 as a network denial of service (ENISA EUVD-2026-25528; BleepingComputer, 2026-05-18; The Hacker News, 2026-05-19). The affected range is kernels from 6.16 before 6.18.23 or 6.19.13, with 7.0 also fixed, and older kernels fall outside it; no source says Debian Stable, RHEL or Ubuntu LTS are unaffected (ENISA EUVD-2026-25528). V12's proof of concept was already public when BleepingComputer reported it on 2026-05-18 (BleepingComputer, 2026-05-18). The interim mitigation BleepingComputer gives blocks the esp4, esp6 and rxrpc modules and breaks IPsec VPNs and AFS (BleepingComputer, 2026-05-18).

    Correction21 Oct 2026 20:44 UTCrun 2026-10-21T1148Z-auditaffected_productsbodyclassificationentitiesevent_dateevidenceheadlinesourcessourcing_notesummarytechniquestitle

    The demo.pdb BadIIS variant is a native IIS module registered in the server module list (globalModules and modules), not an ISAPI filter or extension loaded from web.config, and a hidden backup copy restores it on each IIS restart. Talos contrasts it with the BadIIS variants of DragonRank and UAT-8099 rather than placing it in their activity, and finds it difficult to attribute the attacks to a single actor; the lwxat alias rests on the builder's authentication string, a configuration parameter and a custom user agent. Talos does not describe how the IIS servers were first compromised.

    The variant is not an ISAPI filter or extension: its installers register the malicious DLL in the globalModules and modules sections of the IIS configuration at server level, and the latest one keeps a hidden backup copy that restores it every time IIS restarts, so the place to look is the server module list and the backup location (Cisco Talos, 2026-05-19). Talos does not place it in DragonRank or UAT-8099 activity; it contrasts it with those groups' BadIIS variants, which used hardcoded C2 and static payloads, assesses it as commodity malware likely sold or shared among several Chinese-speaking cybercrime groups, and says the attacks are difficult to attribute to a single actor (Cisco Talos, 2026-05-19). The "lwxat" alias rests on the builder's authentication string, a configuration parameter and a custom user agent, and Talos does not say how attackers first reach the IIS servers (Cisco Talos, 2026-05-19).

    Datadog analyses TeamPCP's open-sourced Shai-Hulud framework: credential and runner-memory theft, IDE and Claude Code hook persistence, OIDC npm publishing and a wipe-on-revocation daemon

    First published 2026-05-15 · open finding →

    Correction21 Oct 2026 20:34 UTCrun 2026-10-21T1148Z-auditaffected_productsbodyclassificationentitiesevent_dateevidenceheadlinesourcessourcing_notesummarytagstechniquestitle

    The repository that published the Shai-Hulud framework source is signed "Love - TeamPCP", and GitHub removed it after it had been forked. Datadog's analysis finds no pull_request_target or expression-injection exploitation in the code: the framework harvests credentials where it already runs, reads Runner.Worker memory, poisons repositories and npm packages with stolen tokens, and uses a workflow's OIDC token only in a release workflow it was hardcoded to target. Datadog's compromise checks include disabling the wipe-on-revocation daemon before rotating tokens.

    The published repository's README reads "Love - TeamPCP" and "Change keys and C2 as needed", its commits are authored as TeamPCP_OSS, and GitHub removed the repository shortly after publication, though not before it was forked, so the code remains available to other actors (Datadog Security Labs, 2026-05-13). Datadog's analysis describes no pull-request exploitation, pull_request_target targeting or expression-injection capability in the code. The framework harvests credentials where it already runs, reads Runner.Worker memory, poisons repository branches and npm packages with stolen tokens, and uses a workflow's own OIDC token only inside a release workflow and for a package it was hardcoded to target, so that path was manually targeted while the token-driven spread is opportunistic (Datadog Security Labs, 2026-05-13).

    The analysis also documents what defenders need for a compromise check: an exit on Russian-locale systems, a daemon that deletes the home directory when a stolen GitHub token is revoked and must be disabled before rotation, and the hook files, commits and npm releases it leaves behind (Datadog Security Labs, 2026-05-13).

    Sources: Datadog Security Labs

    CVE-2026-45793, PHP Composer: GitHub Actions and GitHub App installation tokens printed in full to CI job logs when GitHub's new token format failed validation (CVSS 7.5)

    First published 2026-05-15 · open finding →

    Correction21 Oct 2026 20:33 UTCrun 2026-10-21T1148Z-auditaffected_productsbodyclassificationcvesentitiesevidenceheadlinekindsourcessourcing_notesummarytagstechniquestitle

    Composer's token leak was triggered on the default path by GitHub's new hyphenated installation-token format failing Composer's validation when it loaded its configuration, not by package-resolution errors, and it concerned GITHUB_TOKEN and GitHub App installation tokens rather than personal access tokens. Hosted-runner tokens usually expired with the failing job, self-hosted runner tokens stay valid for up to 24 hours and App tokens for up to an hour, and GitHub rolled the format change back on 2026-05-13. No source links the bug to Mini Shai-Hulud, and the advisory rates it High 7.5 with fixes in 2.9.8, 2.2.28 and 1.10.28.

    The leak was not tied to package-resolution errors: GitHub's new format for GitHub App installation tokens contains a hyphen, fails Composer's character validation when its configuration loads, and the exception prints the full token to stderr, so any workflow with such a token in Composer's auth hit it on the default path, including the auto-injected GITHUB_TOKEN (Packagist, 2026-05-13). Classic ghp_ personal access tokens are not affected by the validation bug itself, though the advisory notes the same leak applies to any future credential that fails validation (Composer, 2026-05-13). Packagist's relevant permission point is that App tokens can carry permissions broader than the workflow's own permissions: declaration, not that every GITHUB_TOKEN can write workflow files (Packagist, 2026-05-13).

    The exposure was bounded: hosted-runner tokens usually expired with the failing job, self-hosted runner tokens stay valid for up to 24 hours and App tokens for up to an hour, and GitHub rolled the format change back on 2026-05-13 before planning a new rollout from 2026-05-18 (Packagist, 2026-05-13). Neither the Packagist post nor the advisory mentions Mini Shai-Hulud or exploitation; the advisory rates the flaw High 7.5, fixed in 2.9.8, 2.2.28 and 1.10.28 (Composer, 2026-05-13).

    Sources: Packagist · Composer (GHSA-f9f8-rm49-7jv2) · CISA KEV catalog

    The Gentlemen RaaS: leaked internal chats show Fortinet and Cisco edge access, NTLM relay, ADCS abuse and EDR-kill tooling, mapped by Check Point Research

    First published 2026-05-14 · open finding →

    Correction21 Oct 2026 20:30 UTCrun 2026-10-21T1148Z-auditaffected_productsbodyclassificationentitiesevidenceheadlinesourcessourcing_notesummarytechniquestitle

    The free Gentlemen decryptor released in April 2026 recovers files only from a memory image of the ransomware process captured while it ran, has no master key and predates the leak, so trying decryption before negotiating does not help most victims; capturing the running locker's memory does. The 1,570 figure is victims of one affiliate's SystemBC server from an earlier Check Point investigation, not hidden ransomware victims, and the group tracks CVE-2024-55591 among other CVEs without the chats showing exploitation. Initial access usually runs through Fortinet and Cisco edge devices by brute force, known vulnerabilities and bought access, the ETW and NTDLL tampering belongs to the locker, and the 8,200 chat lines are DynaRisk's count.

    The decryptor Bedrock Safeguard released in April 2026 does not let victims recover without payment in general: it extracts per-file keys from a memory image of the ransomware process captured while it was running, is best-effort and may be partial, and without such an image cannot recover files, because there is no master key (Bedrock Safeguard, 2026-04-21); the group issued a same-day patch (BankInfoSecurity, 2026-05-11). The 1,570 figure is the victim count on one affiliate's SystemBC command-and-control server from an earlier Check Point investigation, and Check Point draws no under-reporting conclusion from it (Check Point Research, 2026-05-13).

    The chats describe initial access usually, not almost exclusively, through edge devices, by brute force, known vulnerabilities and bought access; CVE-2024-55591 is one of the CVEs the group tracks for potential use without the chats showing exploitation, no CWMP or TR-069 vector appears, ETW patching and NTDLL unhooking are locker upgrades rather than functions of the EDR-blocking tools, and the certificate-services enumeration runs through NetExec rather than certutil (Check Point Research, 2026-05-13). The 8,200 chat lines are DynaRisk's count (BankInfoSecurity, 2026-05-11), and Check Point lists ZeroPulse as a remote-access framework the group uses without describing its internals (Check Point Research, 2026-05-13).

    Sources: Check Point Research · BankInfoSecurity · Bedrock Safeguard (gentlemen-decryptor)
